Disassembly: FNX 4.13A NON-PCT.bin
=====================================

Source: same product family/hardware as dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.bin, a different
        model/build - firmware v4.13A, "NON-PCT" per the file's own name
        (i.e. NOT the Model PCT hot air resin dryer the other dump is
        from). Provided as a comparison reference. See
        fnx-4.13a-non-pct.summary.md for chip/content identification and
        fnx-4.13a-non-pct.messages.md for the full display-message table.
        No separate protocol.md - the SPI CCP frame-builder routines
        weren't relocated/re-verified for this build (see summary.md).

Architecture: Intel MCS-51 (8051-family, likely 8052 variant - same
              Timer 2 vector evidence as the PCT build)

Range disassembled: $0000-$EDFD (the used region - the file's remaining
                     ~7% is unprogrammed $FF fill, not disassembled here)

Method: linear-sweep disassembly (decodes every byte in address order as
        an instruction, does not trace actual code flow) - same tool and
        same limitations as dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.disassembly.txt. Any
        embedded data mixed in with code (lookup tables, the message
        table) will be misdecoded as bogus instructions wherever it
        appears.

Format: <address>  <raw bytes>  <mnemonic + operands>  [; inline comment]
        Bit addresses shown as byte.bit (e.g. $20.3); direct addresses
        $80 and above shown by SFR name where recognized, otherwise $xx.

Relationship to dryer-fnx-4.20-pct.bin: confirmed via direct diff of both
full disassemblies that this build shares a substantial amount of
low-level bootstrap/ISR code BYTE-FOR-BYTE with the PCT build at the SAME
addresses - the reset vector, the full interrupt vector table, and the
External INT0 keypad bit-serial shift-register handler ($0199-$0268ish).
Application-level code (NVRAM defaults, the message table, the SPI CCP
protocol handling, PID/cam parameters) is NOT at the same addresses and
was independently re-traced for this file - see the "Relationship to the
PCT build" section of summary.md for what was and wasn't re-verified.

External memory map (identified so far, same chip assignments as PCT):
        $C000-$C7FF  M48T02-70 TIMEKEEPER NVRAM (2KB, battery-backed).
                     Control+clock registers at $C7F8-$C7FF. Read routine
                     at $56E4, write/reset routine at $576F (see below).
        $A000-$A7FF  volatile external RAM (zeroed at reset, same as PCT)

Code-space data tables identified:
        $DFAC-$E662  display message table, 191 entries, 9 bytes/entry -
                     see fnx-4.13a-non-pct.messages.md

Routines identified/annotated in this pass:
        $002E   Reset/startup
        $0148   Serial (UART) TI/RI interrupt handling (identical to PCT)
        $0199   External INT0 - keypad dispatch (identical to PCT)
        $01ED   Keypad bit-serial shift-in (identical to PCT)
        $4360   UART/timer init (SCON=$50, mode 1, 8-bit, REN=1)
        $56E4   NVRAM TIMEKEEPER read
        $576F   NVRAM TIMEKEEPER write/reset
        $57B2   NVRAM TIMEKEEPER read-stability-check (not traced in full)
        $DFAC   Display message table (data, not code)

=====================================


      ; ======================================================================
      ; INTERRUPT VECTOR TABLE

      ; ======================================================================
      ; DATA, NOT CODE: display message table, base $DFAC, 9 bytes/entry
      ; (8-char field + $00), 191 entries ($DFAC-$E662). See
      ; fnx-4.13a-non-pct.messages.md for the full extracted catalog.
      ; Same 9-byte-stride structure as the PCT build's $E460 table, at a
      ; different base address and with 2 fewer entries. Everything from
      ; here to $E662 will misdecode as nonsense instructions under this
      ; linear-sweep disassembler - expected, not a bug.
      ; ======================================================================
